------------------------------

This creates a spatial inconsistency – Simeon’s testimony places Gavelle and the President on the roof, while the security footage shows only Gavelle arriving and no departure. Simeon’s account suggests a simultaneous presence, while the footage indicates a sequence of events that doesn’t align.

**Step 3:** Explicitly stating the rule: Rule 5 highlights the contradiction. Simeon’s testimony places Gavelle on the roof (as seen in the security footage), making her a suspect according to the rule. However, the lack of footage showing her leaving contradicts the implication of her being present at the time of the murder.

**Step 4:** Explanation of spatial reasoning: The contradiction arises from a discrepancy between a witness’s observation (Simeon) and a recorded event (security footage). Simeon’s account provides a simultaneous presence, while the footage suggests a sequence of events where Gavelle arrives but doesn’t depart. This difference in spatial positioning creates the inconsistency.

Evidence 10 (Police Report) states that traces of poison were found in the victim’s coffee cup, and Evidence 10 also states that no poison was found on Dahlia’s person. However, Testimony 3 from Dahlia states that she and Phoenix met on August 27th in the courthouse basement. Evidence 7 (Phoenix's Testimony) states that Phoenix shows the bottle necklace to everyone, implying a connection to the victim and the poison. This suggests a direct link between Phoenix and the poison, which contradicts the police report’s assertion that no poison was found on Dahlia. Furthermore, Evidence 6 (Phoenix's Testimony) describes the victim falling on his umbrella, creating a scenario where he could have easily spilled the poison into his coffee.

**Step 2:** **Check if any generated rules** from the list above apply to this contradiction

*   **Rule 10:** If the victim ingested poison then the poison was in his coffee. This rule directly addresses the conflicting accounts of poison ingestion.
*   **Rule 3:** If Phoenix shows the bottle necklace then he was near the victim. This rule could be relevant if the necklace is connected to the poison.

**Step 3:** **If applicable rules exist,** explicitly state which rule(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Rule 10 directly states that if the victim ingested poison, it was in his coffee. The police report indicates the poison was *in* the coffee, but the necklace and Phoenix’s testimony suggest a potential link between Phoenix and the poison, creating a contradiction. Rule 3 supports this by suggesting Phoenix was near the victim, potentially involved in the poisoning.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed rules apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial relationship between the victim, the poison, and Phoenix. The police report places the poison in the victim’s coffee, while Phoenix’s testimony and the necklace suggest a possible connection between Phoenix and the poison. The umbrella incident described by Phoenix could have facilitated the spill of the poison into the coffee.
